and positive Hubble rate permit the smooth ODE to reach $A=1/3$ in finite
proper time. At $\omega=10^9$, combining the past and future estimates gives

$$
\left|\frac{A^3E}{E_0}-1\right|<3.416041\times10^{-8},
\qquad A\geq\frac{1}{3}.
$$

The light cone also needs a causal check. The original parent cap's
pole-to-boundary angle exceeds $2.83$. Its entire future conformal angle
is below $11/21$, and its backward angle to $A=1/3$ is below $.912$.
A past emitter is at angle $\eta_o+\eta_p$ from the receiver's pole; its
future domain to the initial slice adds another $\eta_p$. Therefore

$$
\eta_o+2\eta_p<\frac{11}{21}+2(.912)
=2.347810<2.83.
$$

Every ray considered here remains in the domain fixed by the homogeneous
initial cap. No signal from an unconstructed wall history is required.
This is a finite past continuation, not a bounce, primordial state-selection
rule or global completion. Two unaveraged $\omega=200$ formulations agree

### 13.21. What this connection closes and what its source still must supply

The connected calculation now runs from a shared inherited scalar through
a controlled Cauchy history to two astronomical distance channels. The
quiet-cap discrepancy is not hidden: its repair requires the actual evolving
source and a proved earlier observer domain. The common comparison
also supplies the previously missing LambdaCDM control.

Four other physical gaps remain. First, the background family's
$(\rho_v,E_0,C,H^2)\mapsto\lambda^2(\rho_v,E_0,C,H^2)$ leaves the fractions
unchanged and sends distances to $\lambda^{-1}$ times their value. This
corresponds to changing supplied dimensional source parameters together;
it is not a transformation selecting their values at fixed couplings.
Neither inheritance nor a magnitude-offset-free likelihood determines an
SI charge or absolute Lambda. Quantum scale selection or an independently
calibrated dimensional input must close that connection.

Second, the radially stable surface-scalar body in Part VI has
$p=\sigma$ and conserved surface shift charge
$Q=4\pi R^2\theta_{,\tau}$, hence
$\sigma=Q^2/(32\pi^2R^4)$. That law does not select $Q$ or inherit it
between membranes. The evolving parent uses constant charged tension
$p=-\sigma$. The two wall actions retain separate identities until a
compatible preparation/coupling is derived.

Third, P001's actual mass envelope still grows 3.35 percent in its
non-nested three-grid family. The independent
[matched-characteristic control](../results/summaries/nsc-atlas-matched-trace-v1.json)
shows that the adapter preserves deliberately compatible strong traces;
it does not repair independently evolved traces or their unresolved active
cell. Its production endpoint remains an unconverged estimate.

Fourth, this scalar/four-form action has no baryon current or photon
distribution. It supplies one scalar excitation, not separate derived
$\Omega_c$, $\Omega_b$ and $\eta_B=n_b/n_\gamma$. The latter is undefined
in this sector, rather than calculated to vanish. Composition, production
and entropy history must enter through an explicit compatible extension.
Those source tasks, together with joint growth and acoustic-scale tests,
are the next physical work. Further precision in a completed background
cannot replace them.

Direct reduction gives $\kappa_4=\kappa_D/V_0$, positive volume kinetic
coefficient $K=d(d+2)/(2\kappa_4)$ and three-form kinetic factor

$$

The two existing powers lie in its kernel. Bulk vacuum and curvature alone

The preceding value 9.7022370093 is conditional on internal dimension

The general rule $T_j=(j+3)^2/32$ gives

$$

Positive initial stiffness permits $d\geq12$ for $j=2$, $d\geq6$ for
$j=3$, $d\geq5$ for $j=4$, $d\geq4$ for $j=5,6$, and $d\geq3$ for

different object from the internal manifold dimension here. The physical
reduction's inherited ratios also hold for every admitted $d$.

This family has an exact ordering. At $x=e^{-2s}$, the stationary equation
is $F(x)=dA-(d+2)Bx+2dT x^{d/2}=0$. Strict convexity for $d>2$, together
with $F(0)>0$, $F(1)=-dE_0/2<0$ and positive growth at infinity, gives

with $s_*<0$.

Set $u=ds$ and $S_0=\rho+T+E_0/2$. The matched potential becomes

$$

$$

Stationarity removes the derivative through the minimum's location.
The two remaining derivatives, at fixed $u$, are

$$

$$

Convexity of the power and exponential proves the signs. Hence the late

Rational bisection of the stationary polynomial in $y=e^{-s}$ and

Strict monotonicity therefore proves that $(12,2)$ uniquely minimizes

### 13.25. Selection mechanisms and the actual evolving-volume distance test

<!-- nsc-claim:volume-selection-route-test -->

Evidence: [five evolutions, entropy ratios and same-action saddle](../results/summaries/nsc-atlas-volume-selection-routes-v1.json),
[physical scope and sources](../docs/nsc-atlas-volume-selection-routes.md),
and [independent checks](../tests/test_nsc_atlas_volume_routes.py).

The five values in Section 13.24 are minima of different parent actions.
Three proposed routes are now tested against that fact. All five matched
starts evolved to $N=6$ approach their own minima and retain their own
$d,j$. The prescribed equations contain no inter-sector dynamics.

[Gibbons--Hawking entropy](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevD.15.2738)
gives $S=3\pi/(G\Lambda)$ at each late de Sitter endpoint. Their relative
entropies, in the preceding table's order, are
$(1,.971042,.912278,.900341,.904390)$. Thus the lowest member has the
highest entropy. The formula supplies no inheritance step that maximizes
it and no accessibility rule between these different actions.

[Brown--Teitelboim](https://doi.org/10.1016/0370-2693%2887%2991190-7) and
[Coleman--De Luccia](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevD.21.3305) use
specified interfaces or interpolating field configurations in a common
theory. Here the four bounce actions from other members to $(12,2)$ are
undefined because that common theory and interface are missing. Endpoint
potentials cannot fix them: a term proportional to $z^4(1-z)^4$ can
change an interpolation's barrier while preserving its endpoint values
and first two derivatives. Introducing that term would require new
physical input, not a derivation from the present inheritance law.

An actual saddle can be calculated within $(12,2)$. Its positive local
minimum $V_f=.2209830242$ is separated from the direction $s\to\infty$
by the maximum $V_b=.3545366775$. Along that direction $V\to0^+$ and
the internal volume grows without bound. The standard
[Hawking--Moss result](https://doi.org/10.1016/0370-2693%2882%2990946-7) gives

$$
B_{\rm HM}=\frac{403.7817410357}{g},\qquad
g=\frac{\kappa_{\rm nat}}{L_{\rm unit}(0)^2}.
$$

The coefficient is $24\pi^2(1/V_f-1/V_b)$. Its canonical barrier
curvature obeys $-V_{ss}/(KH_b^2)=3.76250309<4$, giving one negative
scalar harmonic and a positive first nonconstant harmonic. This meets
the standard homogeneous saddle's
[negative-mode criterion](https://link.aps.org/accepted/10.1103/PhysRevD.95.123502).
It neither selects another discrete member nor calculates an absolute
rate: $g$, the determinant and the higher-dimensional completion remain
unspecified. It does show why the lowest stable listed member cannot be
declared terminal solely from that list.

The route dispositions are therefore specific: attractors preserve the
different sectors; entropy orders them without a selection law; and
cross-member tunnelling lacks its common action. None is evidence against
nesting. None presently supplies the requested parameter-free selection.

<!-- nsc-claim:evolving-volume-shared-ruler -->

Evidence: [committed-method full-covariance comparison](../results/summaries/nsc-atlas-volume-observations-v1.json),
[observer convention and methods](../docs/nsc-atlas-volume-selection-routes.md),
and [null-quadrature and synthetic-recovery checks](../tests/test_nsc_atlas_volume_observations.py).

The unselected $(12,2)$ member is nevertheless carried forward as a
conditional observation model. The volume and matter exchange energy;
substituting only the smaller vacuum into the old dust formula would
omit that dynamics. We integrate the homogeneous averaged equations and
their null distance over the entire emitter range, using Einstein-frame
metric redshift and fixed luminosity standards. This convention does not
derive the varying-mass model's photon/detector microphysics or a common
three-region causal domain.

The constant late floor and the additional volume contribution are

$$
\Omega_\Lambda=\frac{V_*}{3H_o^2},\qquad
\Omega_s=\frac{Kv_o^2/2+V(s_o)-V_*}{3H_o^2},\qquad
\Omega_\Lambda+\Omega_m+\Omega_k+\Omega_s=1.
$$

The same 1322 supernova rows and all 13 DESI entries use their full
covariances. Both models fit one shape, one magnitude intercept and one
shared ruler. The whole shape posterior is propagated, with positive

### 13.26. Survival ranking and the curvature that must be inherited

<!-- nsc-claim:volume-lifetime-ordering -->

Evidence: [five lifetime coefficients and CDL checks](../results/summaries/nsc-atlas-volume-lifetime-v1.json),
[proof, assumptions and source references](../docs/nsc-atlas-lifetime-curvature.md),
and [symbolic and rational controls](../tests/test_nsc_atlas_volume_lifetime.py).

At fixed flux, the common normalization $B_i=b_i/g$ permits a comparison of leading
[Hawking--Moss](https://doi.org/10.1016/0370-2693%2882%2990946-7)
escape exponents. The resulting values are:

| Member (d,j) | Late vacuum coefficient | HM coefficient b | Regular compact CDL |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| (12,2) | 9.702237009265 | 403.781741036 | Absence unresolved |
| (6,3) | 9.991573647602 | 369.001286854 | Excluded below |
| (5,4) | 10.635176064650 | 326.046593819 | Excluded below |
| (4,5) | 10.776179311559 | 314.036386367 | Excluded below |
| (3,7) | 10.727932243144 | 305.543559374 | Excluded below |

The top negative-curvature ratios are all below four. This alone does
not exclude a distant CDL branch, as the
[global necessary condition](https://arxiv.org/html/gr-qc/0311040)
makes explicit. A stronger identity applies to four of these potentials.
For canonical $\phi=\sqrt{K}s$, put $y=\phi'/a$, $h=a'/a$ and
$Z=h/10+13\phi'/20$. The regular Euclidean equations imply

$$
0=\int a^5\left[(y'+Zy)^2+
\left(G+\frac{39}{100}(h+3\phi'/2)^2\right)y^2\right]d\xi,
$$

$$
G=V_{\phi\phi}+\frac{13}{10}V+\frac{13}{20}V_\phi.
$$

The boundary term vanishes as $a^4$ at each regular pole. Positive $G$
everywhere therefore forces $\phi'=0$, excluding all nonconstant regular
compact O(4) bounces, including thick and oscillating profiles. The
square-completion constants are mathematical choices and add no physical
parameter. For the exponential potentials, a convex one-variable expression
gives the global sign; exact rational bounds certify positivity for
$(6,3),(5,4),(4,5),(3,7)$. The retained heavy scalar is also forced to its
minimum by its positive Euclidean mass identity. Flux-changing membranes,
omitted internal modes and singular instantons are outside this scalar-bounce result.

For $(12,2)$ this certificate is inconclusive. Independent regular-pole
shots are matched at the equator. Eight bounded starting pairs approach
the HM solution and produce no admitted nonconstant root. This finite
search is not a proof of absence, so no reference CDL action is invented.

A precise survival theorem can nevertheless be stated. Assume HM-only
rates $\Gamma_i=A_i(g)e^{-b_i/g}$ with positive subexponential prefactors,
positive initial populations and no replenishment. Conditional survivors
then have weights

$$
p_i(t)=\frac{n_i(0)e^{-\Gamma_i t}}
{\sum_k n_k(0)e^{-\Gamma_k t}}.
$$

For sufficiently small common $g$, the largest $b_i$ is the smallest
rate, so its conditional survivor weight tends to one at late times.
Under this survival rule the HM approximation selects $(12,2)$.
Action ratios are independent of $g$, but actual lifetime ratios retain
$\exp[(b_i-b_j)/g]$ and prefactors. The unresolved reference CDL channel
prevents an unconditional full-decay selection theorem. If any such
reference channel still had coefficient above 369.001286854, the
reference would retain its leading lifetime advantage over these four.

<!-- nsc-claim:curvature-matter-obstruction -->

Evidence: [inherited curvature audit and exact energy bound](../results/summaries/nsc-atlas-curvature-mechanisms-v1.json),
[derivation](../docs/nsc-atlas-lifetime-curvature.md),
and [curvature controls](../tests/test_nsc_atlas_curvature_mechanisms.py).

The three proposed flattening routes have different missing inputs.
[Gott](https://doi.org/10.1038/295304a0) and
[Bucher--Goldhaber--Turok](https://arxiv.org/html/hep-ph/9411206) supply
an open-bubble geometry and post-nucleation inflation. An open origin
starts with curvature domination; small observed curvature requires a
specified subsequent expansion and exit history. That history has not
been inherited by this source. The oscillating-scalar dust phase itself
has $|\Omega_k|$ growing approximately as $a$ during matter domination.
Pure nesting similarity $a\to ra$, $H\to H/r$ leaves $\Omega_k$ invariant.

For the actual prepared volume model, there is a stronger restriction:

$$
\frac{|\Omega_k|}{\Omega_m}=\frac{3C}{E_0}e^{N+6s}.
$$

Total energy decreases from $21/32$. Rational exponential bounds give
$V(-1/12)>21/32$, so $s>-1/12$ along this expanding preparation.
Consequently, for $N\geq0$,

$$
\frac{|\Omega_k|}{\Omega_m}>0.11051717,
\qquad |\Omega_k|\leq0.002\ \Longrightarrow\ \Omega_m<0.01809674.
$$

Late acceleration does dilute curvature, but it also removes the required
matter. The first crossing $\Omega_k=-.002$ is at $N=2.52065449$,
where $\Omega_m=.00109975$. At $N=6$ the curvature is
$-1.8998373\times10^{-6}$ and matter fraction $3.2008896\times10^{-8}$.
These are trajectory values, not a selected present epoch. None of these
three routes derives near-flatness together with the needed matter in the
current preparation. A changed dimensionless source ratio or a specified
matter-producing process after dilution is required.

and [declared method and observer convention](../docs/nsc-atlas-lifetime-curvature.md).

To isolate the role of curvature, impose $C=0$ before fitting and retain

| Quantity | Closed volume | Imposed flat volume | Flat LambdaCDM |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |

| Difference from flat LambdaCDM | +39.77234 | +16.68945 | 0 |
| Fitted quantities | 3 | 3 | 3 |
| Constant vacuum fraction | .63219488 | .57725899 | .69630912 |
| Additional volume fraction | .15392907 | .14332640 | 0 |

The combined-width standardized difference is +3.97366. The corresponding

Flattening substantially improves this conditional comparison, but does

### 13.27. The wall, the exit state and the actual pressure history

<!-- nsc-claim:wall-exit-preparation-map -->

Evidence: [wall projection and HM-state checks](../results/summaries/nsc-atlas-wall-exit-v1.json),
[physical dependency map](../docs/nsc-atlas-wall-exit-preparation.md),
and [independent identities](../tests/test_nsc_atlas_wall_exit_preparation.py).

The next connection must supply curvature and the volume history together.
The action fixes equations; a physical preparation must fix the state
and the wall data from which those equations evolve. The computed HM
saddle, a reference Cauchy slice and a material wall cannot be identified
without that connection.

First distinguish relaxed vacua from the actual initial stress. The
displacement fluxes are $(3/2,1,1/2,0)$; their relaxed vacuum densities
are $(9/32,1/8,1/32,0)$. The supplied uniform scalar is at the
grandchild minimum with zero momentum, giving middle and parent total
densities $U_i=7/32$, $U_o=21/32$. With $k=\sigma/2=1/10$, the old
comoving Israel projection gives

$$
\beta_-=-\frac{M}{kR^2}+\frac{(U_o-U_i+3k^2)R}{6k},\qquad
H_0^2=\frac{\beta_-^2-1+2M/R+U_iR^2/3}{R^2},\qquad
C=\frac{U_o}{3}-H_0^2.
$$

At the supplied $R=1.9$, $M=.12$,

$$
\frac{C}{E_0}=0.06073733964695,\qquad
\Omega_{k,\mathrm{initial}}=-0.116222359874.
$$

That reproduces the reference preparation, whose later fitted curvature
is -.08120633. It does not select a new flattened value. At the same
mass, tension and field state, $R=2$ instead gives $C/E_0=-.0800462963$
and initial curvature fraction +.12066439. The zero occurs at
$R=1.9412508781$; choosing it because flatness is desired would be an
extra selection condition.

The new volume wall also requires $K[\partial_n s]=\sigma_s$,
including the collar's gradient stress. Its jump is -1/70 at $s=0$
and -.00996677 at the HM location. A uniform volume field supplies neither
collar. Furthermore, in a
[CDL open interior](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevD.21.3305), the wall
need not comove with the cosmological slice. The equation $H=\dot R/R$
cannot be transferred from the old projection without that assumption.
The [open-inflation construction](https://arxiv.org/html/hep-ph/9411206)
therefore still needs its actual exit state and reference epoch here.

The computed vacuum HM saddle has $s_b=.06000053504182$, $V_s=0$,
zero velocity and no matter excitation. Its exact classical continuation
stays at the top. In a constant-H de Sitter patch a perturbation grows
with $\lambda/H_b=.9520405974$, but its departure time depends on the
seed; a decade changes the linear departure by 2.41858 e-folds. No
physical seed or unique rolling e-fold count was calculated by finding
the saddle.

Loading the task's $E_i=3/8$ at that same point defines a different,
finite-slice preparation. It gives

$$
\ddot s_i=\frac{6E_i}{84}=\frac{3}{112}>0.
$$

For $s\geq s_b$, the potential has $V_s\leq0$. At zero velocity its
force is nonnegative, so this loaded state cannot turn into negative
velocity and reach the minimum on the left. It rolls toward the
decompactifying side. This is a branch obstruction in the stated source,
not a general objection to a different inherited preparation.

Holding actual starting energy fixed requires

$$
E(N,s)=E_i\exp[-3N-6(s-s_i)].
$$

Preserving the old adiabatic action or the
old coherent field would instead give initial HM-location energies
.26162778 or .18253092. Those prescriptions are not silently exchanged.

<!-- nsc-claim:volume-preparation-pressure-controls -->

Evidence: [retained preparation comparisons and pressure history](../results/summaries/nsc-atlas-exit-preparations-v1.json),
[methods and limits](../docs/nsc-atlas-wall-exit-preparation.md),
and [state and continuity checks](../tests/test_nsc_atlas_wall_exit_preparation.py).

Writing $T=Kv^2/2$, the volume component relative to $V_*$ has

$$
\rho_s=T+V-V_*,\quad p_s=T-V+V_*,\quad
w_s=\frac{p_s}{\rho_s},\qquad
\dot\rho_s+3H(\rho_s+p_s)=6Ev.
$$

Matter loses the same source term. Thus the pressure ratio differs
from the effective dilution parameter,
$w_{s,\mathrm{eff}}=w_s-2Ev/(H\rho_s)$. At zero component density
$w_s$ is undefined; the canonical kinetic norm remains positive.
The combined volume-plus-vacuum pressure ratio is $(T-V)/(T+V)$.

For the current matched flat fit, the actual pressure history is:

| Redshift z | Volume w_s | Combined w_DE | Volume fraction |
| ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 0 | -.886736 | -.977471 | .143326 |
| .25 | -.990120 | -.997903 | .122643 |
| .5 | -.999998 | -1.000000 | .095024 |
| 1 | -.896920 | -.976132 | .059570 |
| 1.5 | -.024749 | -.600811 | .079416 |
| 2 | +.627429 | +.165435 | .171422 |

The present volume fraction therefore does not describe its entire
observable history. To test preparation directly, the same likelihood
is applied to the computed HM location and to the computed minimum,
each with fixed actual $E_i=3/8$ and zero starting volume velocity.
Neither start is fitted. Flatness and the recovered old C are controls;
no new wall-compatible post-exit curvature is claimed.

| Start | Curvature control | Joint chi-square | Delta vs flat LambdaCDM | Present volume fraction |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Matched | Flat | 1207.86625 | +16.68945 | .143326 |
| HM location + matter | Flat | 1330.53101 | +139.35422 | .303996 |
| HM location + matter | Old C | 1392.91214 | +201.73534 | .326298 |
| Minimum + matter | Flat | 1237.06508 | +45.88828 | .012710 |
| Minimum + matter | Old C | 1277.82738 | +86.65058 | .015530 |

All fits have three observational quantities and use the same shared
ruler and full covariances. Flat LambdaCDM has chi-square 1191.17680.
For the HM-location flat control,
$(\Omega_\Lambda,\Omega_s,\Omega_m,\Omega_k)=(.495982,.303996,.200022,0)$;
with old C the tuple is $(.532212,.326298,.204525,-.063035)$.
The corresponding posterior sums $3(\Omega_\Lambda+\Omega_s)$ are
$2.399845\pm.010551$ and $2.575431\pm.008741$, giving combined-width
differences +14.17 and +22.03 from $2.054\pm.022$. On this runaway
branch $V_*$ is a reference offset, not its future vacuum.

The minimum-start flat control lowers the present volume fraction to
.012710, but raises the constant-vacuum fraction to .745006 and reduces
matter to .242284. Its posterior dark-energy sum is
$2.273023\pm.014245$, or +8.36 combined-width units. With old C the
tuple is $(.814085,.015530,.254214,-.083829)$ and the posterior sum is
$2.488710\pm.011674$, or +17.45 units. Component covariance is included.
At flatness the sum is exactly $1-\Omega_m$; reducing one component
alone does not lower total dark energy.

![One preparation must determine both the wall curvature and the volume pressure history.](../docs/figures/nsc-atlas-preparation-map.png)

At $z=2$ the minimum-start flat source has volume fraction .226330,
despite its small present fraction. Its earlier kinetic/pressure history
helps explain why the full fit worsens. Numerical settings change joint
chi-square by at most $5.6\times10^{-9}$.

These are reference-slice continuations, including earlier emitters. The
fitted $N=0$ slices lie near redshifts .59--.73 in the new controls;
higher-redshift data use the backward continuation. They cannot be
advertised as post-nucleation-only histories with that slice as birth.
The requested genuine-exit refit therefore remains unfulfilled.

Finally, rounding is explicit: the displayed six-decimal pair gives
$3(.577259+.143326)=2.161755$. The unrounded best-fit sum is
2.1617561739988 and the posterior mean 2.1616589757622. They are
different reported quantities. The next physical calculation must supply
a compatible parent/child transition and its Lorentzian profiles and wall
collar. Evolve that state to the reference event $E=3/8$, if it exists,
and carry its volume, velocity and curvature forward without resetting
them. The reference density must not be mistaken for a derived birth state.
Every emitter must then lie in the constructed history before the next fit.

### 13.28. A mass-free nucleation radius and the matter-reference gate

<!-- nsc-claim:parke-mass-free-radius -->

Evidence: [constant-vacuum bubble and mass balance](../results/summaries/nsc-atlas-parke-bubble-v1.json),
[source scope and unit dictionary](../docs/nsc-atlas-parke-nucleation.md),
and [independent junction and limit checks](../tests/test_nsc_atlas_parke_nucleation.py).

The proposed replacement of the supplied radius and seed mass has a
definite answer within a constant-vacuum thin-wall model. Reusing
[Parke (1983)](https://doi.org/10.1016/0370-2693%2883%2991376-X),
which extends [Coleman--De Luccia](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevD.21.3305),
take $U_i=7/32$, $U_o=21/32$, $\sigma=1/5$ and $\kappa=1$. The
ordinary positive-cap branch gives

$$
R_c^{-2}=\left(\frac{U_o-U_i}{3\sigma}\right)^2
+\frac{\kappa(U_i+U_o)}{6}+\frac{(\kappa\sigma)^2}{16}
=\frac{39169}{57600},
$$

$$
R_c=\frac{240}{\sqrt{39169}}=1.212662645008\ L_{\rm unit}(0),
\qquad M_i=M_o=0.
$$

Neither the old 1.9 nor the old .12 enters that calculation. The new
radius is .63824350 times the supplied one. Both radii are in the
original model unit. For historical comparison only, the old
$L_{\rm ref}(0)=6.6260770761L_{\rm unit}(0)$ gives ratios .18301366
and .28674583. That old curvature ruler was derived from the removed
preparation and is not a new prediction. The independent false-vacuum
ruler gives $R_c/\sqrt{3/(\kappa U_o)}=.56717102$.

At the turning point the signed normal factors are
$\beta_i=187/\sqrt{39169}$ and $\beta_o=163/\sqrt{39169}$.
They satisfy the unsquared junction exactly. Regular homogeneous vacua
have zero Schwarzschild seed offsets, and their mass balance is

$$
0=-\frac{(U_o-U_i)R_c^3}{6}
+\frac{\sigma R_c^2\beta_i}{2}
-\frac{\sigma^2 R_c^3}{8}.
$$

The terms are -.130030971151, +.138947380602 and -.008916409450.
The interior geometric Misner--Sharp mass is nevertheless .065015485576 because
it contains vacuum energy. Zero seed mass alone does not determine the
homogeneous scalar state or imply zero total energy.

The auxiliary bubble has wall trajectory
$R(\tau_w)=R_c\cosh(\tau_w/R_c)$. Its pure-vacuum open interior has
$a(t)=\sinh(H_it)/H_i$, $H_i^2=7/96$, and
$\Omega_k(t)=\mathrm{sech}^2(H_it)$, with birth limit +1. Wall proper
time and interior cosmic time differ. A finite turning radius is not a
matter-defined FRW reference scale.

<!-- nsc-claim:parke-source-reheating-admission -->

Evidence: [vacuum, reheating and energy-budget admission](../results/summaries/nsc-atlas-parke-reheating-admission-v1.json)
and [exact matter-invariance checks](../tests/test_nsc_atlas_parke_nucleation.py).

This geometric solution must still belong to the same action before it
can replace the preparation. The stated densities were values of an
excited scalar snapshot. At its supplied field, with $y=\omega\phi$,
the middle and exterior derivatives are

$$
U_y=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{4},\qquad U_y=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}.
$$

They are nonzero. The relaxed scalar densities are instead 1/8 and 9/32.
The volume adds another incompatibility: its well minimum
$V_*=.220983024213$ exceeds the stipulated interior density .21875.
The actual coupled wall also has $\sigma(s)=.2e^{-6s}$ and requires
its volume-gradient profile. The closed-form radius does not supply
that profile or an actual scalar turning point for these endpoints.

For an empty retained matter field in the homogeneous post-wall region
at fixed flux and without incoming sources, the supplied equations give

$$
\dot E+(3H+6\dot s)E=0,\qquad
E=E_i\exp[-3(N-N_i)-6(s-s_i)].
$$

Thus $E_i=0$ remains zero for any roll of $s$. The exact classical
$\chi=\Pi=0$ solution has the same invariance. This does not replace
a CDL turning point by the HM top, and it is not a claim that all quantum
reheating is impossible. It establishes that the present classical
source does not create the requested reference event from that empty state.

Counting volume oscillation energy as matter still requires an adequate
budget. With any internal energy-conserving transfer to positive dust,
an expanding homogeneous branch obeys

$$
\dot\rho_{\rm total}=-3H(K\dot s^2+E)\leq0.
$$

The literal thin-wall child density $7/32$ is below the matter target
$3/8$ even before a vacuum is retained. A rest exit that can arrive at
the well with the requested matter requires at least

$$
V_{\rm exit}\geq V_*+\frac38=0.595983024213.
$$

The excess available within the computed minimum-to-barrier interval is
only .133553653265 before expansion losses. This bound applies to that
interval; it does not assign a value to an unknown CDL exit. On the
potential's rising left side the necessary energy would require
$s\leq-.07885602698$, which is an energy threshold, not a derived
turning point. The parent difference $U_o-V_*=.435266975787$ could
cover the budget, but its delivery into the child has not been calculated.

Ordinary on-shell reheating into the retained massive scalar is also
closed near the minimum: $m_s=1.477737463$ while
$2m_\chi=2.476949780\times10^9$ in the same inverse-length units.
Nonperturbative and curved-background quantum production are not computed;
no new phenomenological reheating rate is inserted.

The result is consequently precise. The stipulated vacuum-wall model
fixes its radius and zero seed masses. Its endpoints fail admission as
vacua of the unchanged scalar/volume source, and its empty classical
matter branch never reaches $E=3/8$. There is no admissible reference
event from which to report a new $C/E_0$, present curvature, $w_s(z)$
or SN+DESI likelihood. No reset state or fictitious fit replaces that
missing event. A compatible vacuum transition and matter-transfer
mechanism must be derived together before this geometry becomes the
requested reheated cosmology. These route failures do not reject nesting.

### 13.29. Three targets from the spectral and regional map

<!-- nsc-claim:three-dimensionless-targets -->

Evidence: [dimensionless calculation and controls](../results/summaries/nsc-atlas-three-targets-v1.json),
[formula meanings and missing connections](../docs/nsc-cosmological-atlas.md#three-numerical-targets-from-the-existing-equations),
and [independent algebra and scale checks](../tests/test_nsc_atlas_three_targets.py).

Douglas's organizing picture asks whether the band of a confined region,
an imbalance between conjugate oscillations, and the response from outside
can yield three numerical predictions. Here the calculation uses only
the existing atlas equations and retained dimensionless inputs. No Planck
length in metres or other CODATA anchor is imported, and no target selects
a depth, source state or energy weight.

The related force-resolution proposal asks whether the frequency- and
momentum-dependent response of one action produces the different interaction
channels seen locally. It is a question about deriving couplings and their
scale dependence. It does not identify strong or weak interactions with
an event horizon, whose role in the atlas is causal access.

For the occupied sphere spectrum, $\omega_n=cn/R$, the highest selected
mode and the current lowest mode give

$$
\ln\frac{\omega_{\max,\rm infl}}{\omega_{\min,\rm now}}
=\ln q_{\max}+\ln\frac{R_{\rm now}}{R_{\rm infl}}.
$$

This assumes common local propagation speed and the angular ground n=1.
A varying speed would add its endpoint ratio. The product
$\omega_{\max}R_{\rm infl}/c=q_{\max}$ belongs to the selected state or
cutoff; the untruncated spectrum has no largest n. With the stated
wavelength formula, the ground wavelength is $2\pi R$. Thus the size
convention must also be explicit.

The RN/PG diagnostic gives a different quantity:

$$
B_{\rm grid}=\frac{\omega_{\rm cutoff}}{\omega_{\rm zero}}
=\frac{r}{\Delta r}\left(1+\frac{|\beta|}{N}\right).
$$

Here N is lapse and the cutoff uses numerical grid spacing. Refining the
spacing by two doubles this band without changing the metric. The
diagnostic alone therefore does not select a physical shortest wavelength.

In the spectral inheritance equation, Lambda_n is a frequency scale.
Frequency multiplication by Omega implies length division by Omega at
fixed c. The associated curvature factor is zeta=Omega squared, as in
the existing dilation implementation. Using the same Omega as a curvature
factor would be a different physical identification. The q=4 binding
candidate gives Omega=3.973074368754331 and ln(Omega)=1.379540195196.
If its frequency map were the cosmological transition and its endpoint
factor were one, the conditional calculation would be:

| Assigned depth | Log band | Miss vs 136.84 (%) | Miss vs 138.26 (%) |
| ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 1 | 1.3795401952 | -98.99186 | -99.00221 |
| 2 | 2.7590803904 | -97.98372 | -98.00443 |
| 100 | 137.9540195196 | +.81410 | -.22131 |

The last row is numerically inside the requested window, but neither
100 levels nor the endpoint factor is selected. The window inverts to
depths 99.19247--100.22180 only under those supplied assumptions. Under
the proposed curvature-factor reading, 200 assigned steps give the same
137.9540195196; that count is equally unselected. The
Parke radius and the late volume vacuum do not supply the missing depth
or band endpoints, and the q=4 spectral candidate has not been identified
with that cosmological source.

The conjugate-mode expansion likewise separates phase from population.
For mode occupations,

$$
\mathcal A=\frac{n_+-n_-}{n_++n_-},\qquad
\eta_B=\mathcal A_b\frac{n_b+n_{\bar b}}{n_\gamma}.
$$

A relative phase cancels from the number expectation values. Equal
occupations give zero imbalance for every phase; occupations 3/4 and 1/4
give 1/2 for the same operator. Neither choice is selected by the geometry.
A baryon/photon prediction additionally needs the physical sector assignment
and photon or entropy normalization. A freely supplied amplitude offset
would not be a derivation of the target 6.1e-10.

Finally, the stipulated Parke densities give Uo/Ui=3. Even an auxiliary
outside energy fraction requires a volume ratio v=V_out/V_in:

$$
f_{\rm out}=\frac{3v}{1+3v},\qquad
\Omega_a=\frac{\kappa\rho_a}{3H^2}.
$$

The first expression is .75 at v=1 and .5 at v=1/3; it is not a
vacuum/clustering/baryon decomposition. The second needs one observer's
effective stress and expansion. A vacuum-only Friedmann limit gives
(1,0,0), formally missing (.685,.265,.049) by (+45.9854%,-100%,-100%).
That is a limiting control, not a present-day abundance prediction.

The actual outputs for all three requested predictions remain unselected,
and their physical percentage misses are undefined. What is missing is a
physical endpoint/transition map, a charged-state preparation and baryon/photon
identification, and an exterior-to-observer stress projection. These are not
necessarily one new scalar each. The numerical targets remain comparisons
rather than substitutes for those maps. At exponential boundaries, logarithms,
dimensionless ratios and compatible regional charts preserve the calculation;
a clamp or coordinate limit is not promoted to a physical endpoint.

### 13.30. Constructing the pen: a phase history on the sphere

<!-- nsc-claim:constructed-pen-geometry -->

Evidence: [geometry and finite measurements](../results/summaries/nsc-atlas-pen-geometry-v1.json),
[construction and proofs](../docs/nsc-atlas-pen-geometry.md),
and [independent geometric and numerical checks](../tests/test_nsc_atlas_pen_geometry.py).

The next step constructs a candidate from two clocks already present in the
(12,2) action. Near its late vacuum, the volume displacement and canonical
scalar displacement are independent linear modes. Removing their common
Hubble damping by multiplying by $a^{3/2}$ gives

$$
\nu_s^2=\frac{V_{ss}}{84}-\frac{3V_*}{4},\qquad
\nu_\chi^2=\omega_0^2e^{-12s_*}-\frac{3V_*}{4}.
$$

One pass is defined as a scalar-mode period, sampling the volume phase.
For x=exp(-2s_star), stationarity gives the exact polynomial
25x^6-140x+112=0. Reduction by that polynomial yields

$$
\delta=2\pi\frac{\nu_s}{\nu_\chi}
=2\pi\sqrt{\frac{475x-468}{32\omega_0^2+75x-84}}
=0.360395008387051
$$

for the retained parent coupling omega0=20. The polynomial is Eisenstein
at7, and the squared frequency ratio is a nonconstant rational fractional-linear
function of its irrational root; the rotation is therefore irrational.
The offset is derived conditional on the supplied coupling, not fitted.
Mapping the sampled phase to a spatial circle is the declared geometric
step. It is a circle fiber of the phase map, not the continuous trajectory
of a particle. Pass k draws

$$
X(u,v)=R(\sin u\cos v,\sin u\sin v,\cos u),\qquad
v_k=k\delta.
$$

Circle orientations are read modulo pi. The independent curve parameter u
and plane orientation v give |X|=R and
area Jacobian $R^2|\sin u|$. The continuous family is the sphere; the
irrational sequence has the same dense closure. Finite curves have zero
area. A radial coordinate would be needed to fill a ball.

![Constructed pen and its computed readouts](figures/nsc-atlas-pen-geometry.png)

*The same clock-defined circle history supplies coverage, a signed phase moment
and a quadratic probe. The orange region retains earlier ink. Assigned
exponential refinement and achieved angular coverage are measured separately;
no curve or parameter is fitted to the targets.*

Give pass k the angular footprint $\epsilon_k=\Omega^{-(k+1)/p}$, with
p=1 for the frequency-scale reading and p=2 for the requested square-root
reading. Old ink persists. The Parke radius can set R, but it cancels from
these angular fractions. The maximum distance to the first n circle planes is

$$
h_n=\frac{\max_i g_i}{2}\geq\frac{\pi}{2n},
$$

where the orientation gaps sum to pi. This is an optimistic resolution
bound because each circle itself was drawn continuously. At 100 passes,
log(1/h_n)=3.7411273; at 200, it is 3.9020327. Their logarithmic misses
from 136.84--138.26 are about -97.29% and -97.18%, respectively, using

or n=200,p=2, but is not achieved by those finite drawings. Reaching that

The missing history link is a stopping event or a rule that branches each

The same late-vacuum action gives physical expansion of .0688557421
e-folds per scalar cycle: 6.8855742 at100 cycles and13.7711484 at200.
A phase cycle is therefore not automatically one Omega inheritance step.
The existing alternative source members omega0=2000 and10^9 give different
offsets, .00360346331762 and7.20692653791e-9; no member is substituted
to improve a comparison. Formation and the physical stop event remain open.

The bound is constructively sharp: M equally spaced meridians rotated
by the accumulated phase have fill distance pi/(2M). Taking
M=ceil(pi/(2*epsilon)) achieves the requested resolution. A branching
refinement can therefore work geometrically, but its many-circle history
and physical stopping event must be supplied by a transition law.

For a precise offset readout, let $z_k=e^{ik\delta}$ mark an oriented

Their normalized vector moment telescopes:

$$
D_n=\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(z_k-z_{k+1})
=\frac{1-e^{in\delta}}{2n},\qquad
|D_n|=\frac{|\sin(n\delta/2)|}{n}.
$$

It gives .00737800095 at 100 passes and .00498029284 at 200, exceeding

with that target. Each pass still has one positive and one negative scalar

The missing asymmetry link is the physical charge-producing process and
its baryon/photon observable.

For the retained ink, union the open geodesic bands of widths epsilon_k.
At $z=|\cos\theta|$, calculate their union longitude length L_n(z) on
the orientation circle of length pi. The drawn fraction is

$$
f_n=\int_0^1\frac{L_n(z)}{\pi}\,dz.
$$

Monotone quadrature and a geometric tail bound give the following finite

The drawn-area enclosures are [.2966405,.2968324] and [.6289221,.6290423].

### 13.31. The inward drawing: golden turns and inherited clocks

<!-- nsc-claim:golden-vortex-comparison -->

The drawing can continue indefinitely while an observer occupies a finite
depth. The [golden-vortex calculation](../docs/nsc-atlas-golden-vortex.md)
constructs that continuation and compares it with the retained action clocks.
For a point x in a unit ball, the same operation

$$
S(x,t)=(\sqrt{1-t^2}\,x,t),\qquad -1\leq t\leq1
$$

maps one ball onto the next-dimensional ball. Applied twice to a line segment,
it fills a three-ball; its boundaries pass through circle and sphere. The two
offsets are retained independent parameters. An inward history is

$$
F_n=R_c q^{-n}{\cal R}_z(n\delta)S(S(x,t_1),t_2).
$$

This gives nested copies for every q>1. Radius-normalized spherical Dirac
operators inherit the same law for every such q, so that invariance supplies
the construction but does not choose its scale ratio.

The golden choice has phi=(1+sqrt(5))/2, delta=2pi/phi^2 and q=phi^4. Its
planar logarithmic spiral shrinks by phi per quarter turn. On the sphere,

$$
\gamma(t)=R_c q^{-t}X(2\pi t,\delta t),\qquad
\gamma(t+1)=q^{-1}{\cal R}_z(\delta)\gamma(t).
$$

Local radial gaps therefore shrink geometrically. Angular coverage remains
a separate question: N points covering a unit sphere have angular covering
radius at least acos(1-2/N). Integer samples of this particular continuous
curve return to the pole; its full direction history has dense closure.
It is the turns, not those isolated integer samples, that draw the curve.

The golden angle is 2.399963229729 versus the action's .360395008387.
The full-turn ratio 6.854101966250 exceeds Omega=3.973074368754 by 72.5138%.
phi^3 differs by 6.6194%; ln(Omega)/ln(phi)=2.866804436955 is not an exact
integer-power link. No physical sampling clock makes these choices equal.

Reusing the stated persistent spherical-band footprint gives:

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| Golden full turns | .1669757270 | .8330242730 | +21.6094% | -46.9918% |

The black-hole comparisons also distinguish scale readouts. Schwarzschild

the corresponding equatorial instability exponent varies with spin; the

These are geodesic offsets, not child-radius ratios. The sources are

The Kasner/BKL connection is exact in its own vacuum approximation:

$$
(p_1,p_2,p_3)=\frac{(-u,1+u,u(1+u))}{1+u+u^2},

At u=phi the bounce cycles (-.3090169944,.5,.8090169944). Its derivative

[Frolov--Markov--Mukhanov](https://doi.org/10.1016/0370-2693%2889%2991114-3)

The [spin-torsion bounce](https://arxiv.org/abs/1110.5019) is another specified

### 13.32. Through the horizon: smooth geometry, charge and an offset

<!-- nsc-claim:smooth-horizon-and-offset -->

A causal horizon is now the proposed handoff. The
[smooth-horizon calculation](../docs/nsc-atlas-smooth-horizon.md) develops
that branch without treating the old material wall as compulsory.
At fixed Ui=7/32 and Uo=21/32, the Parke formula gives

$$
R_c^{-2}=\frac{(U_o-U_i)^2}{9\sigma^2}
+\frac{\kappa(U_i+U_o)}{6}+\frac{\kappa^2\sigma^2}{16}.
$$

Thus Rc tends to zero as tension tends to zero; 1.212662645 is not retained
as a finite horizon. The flux equation also requires continuous q when the
membrane current is removed. Keeping a charge jump while deleting only
tension would still leave an interface source.

The existing effective-action minimum has Lambda_star=.220983024213.
Its constant fields admit a smooth Kottler metric:

$$
ds^2=-f\,dv^2+2dv\,dr+r^2d\Omega^2,\qquad
f=1-\frac{2m}{r}-\frac{\Lambda_*r^2}{3}.
$$

The determinant is -r^4 sin(theta)^2. At a horizon f=0 it remains regular
away from the ordinary angular-coordinate poles. An infaller has

$$
\frac{dr}{d\tau}=-\sqrt{E^2-f},\qquad
\frac{dv}{d\tau}=\frac{1}{E+\sqrt{E^2-f}}.
$$

At the crossing these become -E and 1/(2E), and the norm stays -1. This
proves that this zero marks the end of the static chart, with continuation
in the same spacetime. It does not by itself create a new independent
dimension or resolve the central r=0 singularity of the m>0 solution.

At m=0 the same-action de Sitter horizon is **3.684522144189 model units**.
Forcing a black-hole root to equal the old Rc would require m=.540652151655;
that is an inversion diagnostic, not a derived mass. Smooth joining of two
constant-vacuum Kottler pieces in one EF normalization gives
[m]=-[Lambda]R^3/6 and [f']=-[Lambda]R, so equal first derivatives require
equal vacuum and mass parameters. Null interfaces use the transverse-curvature
conditions of [Barrabes--Israel](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevD.43.1129)
and [Poisson](https://arxiv.org/abs/gr-qc/0207101). A sourced smooth transition
remains a separate field problem.

![Smooth continuation, an oriented offset and two horizon responses](figures/nsc-atlas-smooth-horizon.png)

Global zero charge can mean opposite charge in two regions. The continuity
equation makes the distinction from no production calculable:

$$
Q(V_2)-Q(V_1)=-\int_{\partial V}J^\mu d\Sigma_\mu
+\int_{\mathcal V}S_B\,dV.
$$

A charge-symmetric state and dynamics on a moving neutral metric still have
zero expected charge in a region preserved by internal charge conjugation.
This does not exclude charge-separated states; it says their selection
requires state or interaction data. A global CPT pairing can exchange regions
and permits Q_minus(-t)=-Q_plus(t) without fixing its magnitude.
[Boyle--Finn--Turok](https://arxiv.org/html/1803.08928v3) supply a specific
pre-/post-bang CPT cosmology, not a general identification of black-hole
horizons with antimatter. [Cohen--Kaplan--Nelson](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/9302210)
combine moving walls with CP-violating interactions and transport. Motion
alone is not that baryogenesis mechanism.

Douglas's offset nevertheless has a precise charged realization. For the
two-component harmonic comparison x=A cos(omega t), y=B cos(omega t-delta),

$$
Q=x\dot y-y\dot x=AB\omega\sin\delta,\qquad
\frac{Q}{E/\omega}=\frac{2AB}{A^2+B^2}\sin\delta.
$$

The ellipse's orientation is its conserved charge. With equal amplitudes,
the action phase gives .352643892877 and the golden angle .675490294262.
These are charge per total oscillator quanta, not baryon/photon eta. The
existing volume and scalar modes have unequal frequencies, so they do not
already constitute this degenerate charged pair. The comparison constructs
the missing object explicitly while retaining its additional field/state
assumption. With common expansion damping, a^3 Q is conserved: density
dilutes without generating an initial imbalance.

The existing neutral quadratic field also has two calculable stress readouts:

$$
\rho=V_*+\frac{\dot\chi^2+\omega^2\chi^2}{2},\qquad
p=-V_*+\frac{\dot\chi^2-\omega^2\chi^2}{2}.
$$

A uniform phase ensemble on a fixed de Sitter background gives
rho_osc=A^2 omega^2/(2a^3) and p_osc=0; the baseline has w=-1. This connects
baseline and oscillation to vacuum-like and matter-like gravity. Their
fractions, charge state and baryon identity are not selected by this split.

Finally, adding a specified null source permits m=m(v) in the same regular
metric. Direct calculation gives

$$
T_{vv}^{\rm null}=\frac{2m'}{\kappa r^2},\qquad
\frac{dR_h}{dv}=\frac{2m'}{1-\Lambda R_h^2},\qquad
T^\mu{}_{\mu}=0.
$$

Decreasing m shrinks the black-hole marginal root and enlarges the
cosmological one. This advanced-coordinate comparison requires negative
ingoing energy for that sign, together with a physical source and exterior
balance. It has not derived the drain rate. These are local marginal roots,
not automatically global event horizons; see
[Nielsen et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/1007.2990). The traceless drain adds no
vacuum Lambda in this construction. It can change a horizon without itself
being dark energy.

The [record](../results/summaries/nsc-atlas-smooth-horizon-v1.json) gives
the exact identities, numerical controls and independent tests. The
charge-symmetric control is zero (-100% against 6.1e-10), while physical
baryon/photon eta remains unselected. The flat pure-vacuum control has
Omega_Lambda=1 (+45.9854% against .685), not a matter-filled-universe fit.
The next bridge is an inherited charge current and state whose stress
produces a smooth, sourced horizon history. That makes the continuation
and the offset testable in the same geometry.

### 13.33. Deriving the charged pair from internal geometry

<!-- nsc-claim:inherited-harmonic-charge -->

The previous offset comparison needed two equal-mass components. The
[inherited-charge calculation](../docs/nsc-atlas-inherited-charge.md) now
derives a candidate pair from the real higher-dimensional scalar already
used in the volume reduction. We explicitly complete its internal Einstein
space as a round S12. The action fixes its scalar curvature, but does not
select roundness among all possible internal geometries. This is a quadratic
harmonic calculation on the effective late vacuum, not a full nonlinear
sixteen-dimensional formation solution.

The curvature term -15 exp(-14s)/2 gives R_gamma=15, hence

$$
a_0^2=\frac{12\cdot11}{15}=\frac{44}{5},\qquad
\lambda_l=\frac{l(l+11)}{a_0^2},\qquad \lambda_1=\frac{15}{11}.
$$

The thirteen real l=1 harmonics are normalized embedding coordinates.
For a rotation in one coordinate plane,

$$
Y_+=\frac{Y_1+iY_2}{\sqrt{2}},\qquad
\delta\Phi=xY_1+yY_2=\psi Y_+^*+\psi^*Y_+,
\qquad \psi=\frac{x+iy}{\sqrt{2}}.
$$

Thus one real higher-dimensional field supplies a complex four-dimensional
mode carrying internal rotational charges +/-1. Six commuting planes
organize the thirteen real harmonics as six complex pairs and one neutral
component. This uses the attributed
[Kaluza-Klein construction](https://arxiv.org/pdf/hep-th/9410046); no new
elementary complex scalar was inserted. Mixed metric components supply
gauge fields when these modes backreact. A finite-mode nonlinear truncation
and identification of this charge with baryon number are not established.

Canonical reduction fixes the pair's mass:

$$
M_1^2(s)=\omega_0^2e^{-12s}+\frac{15}{11}e^{-14s}.
$$

At s_star=-.035646782553 and the retained omega0=20, this is
615.774153968986, so M1=24.814797076925 in model units. The internal gradient
contributes 2.246132580192 to the mass squared. This degeneracy is geometric;
it does not equate the previously unequal volume and matter frequencies.

The round geometry also has a proper rotation flipping coordinates n2 and
n13. Its determinant is +1; it conjugates the selected charge generator
while preserving both the metric and oriented top-form flux. The supplied
flux therefore does not select a sign of this charge. Neither does the
ancestor count supply an internal rotational current.

Both components cross a neutral smooth horizon with the same radial operator:

$$
f u''+(f'-2i\omega)u'
-\left(\frac{f'}{r}+M_1^2+\frac{l(l+1)}{r^2}\right)u=0.
$$

At a simple horizon its powers are 0 and 2i omega/f'(r_h). The regular
branch has an arbitrary complex amplitude. Regular crossing transports
the field but does not select its phase or occupation.

On the stationary de Sitter parent, the regular Euclidean Gaussian
preparation has positive spectral coefficients H_star^2 L(L+3)+M1^2.
Its two component covariances agree, with zero cross covariance and uniform
phase. This is a specified invariant state-selection rule, not a claim that
the action alone selects all initial data. [Allen's state analysis](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevD.32.3136)
distinguishes Euclidean regularity from de Sitter symmetry alone.
The [Gibbons-Hawking](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevD.15.2738) static-patch
temperature is H_star/(2pi)=.043195545274.

For a stationary mode, write z=exp(-2pi omega/H_star). The local charge
difference of its two thermal populations obeys

$$
P(Q=k)=\frac{1-z}{1+z}z^{|k|},\qquad
\langle Q\rangle=0,\qquad
\operatorname{Var}(Q)=\frac{2z}{(1-z)^2}.
$$

This retains fluctuations rather than identifying zero mean with zero in
every realization. At the illustrative detector gap omega=M1, ln(z) is
-574.475838182 and the one-mode charge RMS is 2.539052865e-125. These are
spectral controls, not a baryon-to-photon ratio or a selected regional
population. A cosmological fluctuation needs mode weights and photon density.

The neutral Bogoliubov pair map gives the exact operator identity

$$
N_{+,\rm out}-N_{-,\rm out}=N_{+,\rm in}-N_{-,\rm in},
\qquad |\alpha|^2-|\beta|^2=1.
$$

More generally, an internal charge-exchange-equivariant transport channel
preserves an invariant state at every nesting depth. Regional charge still
obeys the boundary-current law; this does not assume that an open region
conserves its charge separately. A biased inherited state can carry an
imbalance, but neutral propagation cannot select that bias from this vacuum.

The [record](../results/summaries/nsc-atlas-inherited-charge-v1.json) gives
zero mean charge asymmetry. If this charge is identified with baryons and
a positive photon density is supplied, eta=0, a -100% miss against 6.1e-10.
The physical baryon/photon ratio remains unselected. The contribution is
the geometric field pair, its explicit horizon equation, and a conditional
state and charge distribution. The remaining connection is an inherited
internal current or charge-asymmetric preparation, together with its
baryon/photon interpretation; assigning a fitted phase would not close it.

## IV. Concentration, conservation and the surrounding reservoir

A dark-matter account needs a physical source that forms structure and accounts for its surroundings. We first calculate admissible scalar concentrations and their conserved supply, then their persistence and spatial compensation, and finally formation from compact disturbances. The route preserves the inherited parent instead of adding an independent halo mass. Different frequencies and preparations are explicit members of the family; they are not exchanged between examples by a change of units.

### 14. The energy supply and formation of structure

The following sections give the worked constructions and their attributed mathematical inputs.

### 14.1. A profile selected by scalar self-gravity

<!-- nsc-claim:scalar-core-profile-selection -->

The operational clock family in Section 15.1 uses a specified profile.
Here a physical selection mechanism is calculated: a real massive scalar can
concentrate through its own gravity and shed radiation, leaving a
coherent core. Gravitational cooling is established prior work of
[Seidel and Suen](https://arxiv.org/abs/gr-qc/9309015). The stationary
equations are also those studied by
[Moroz, Penrose and Tod](https://doi.org/10.1088/0264-9381/15/9/019),
with time-dependent examples in
[Harrison, Moroz and Tod](https://arxiv.org/html/math-ph/0208046).

The weak-field real-scalar reduction with positive vacuum curvature
is supplied by [Fodor, Forgacs and Mezei](https://arxiv.org/html/1007.0388).
Here its complex envelope represents one classical real field.
It introduces neither a new charged species nor a quantum
state-reduction law. We independently solve the profile and its
finite local evolution, then construct a sourced atlas initial slice.

First distinguish a coupling from a unit convention. At fixed mixing,
fluxes and tension, the common field at the grandchild minimum gives

$$
\phi_0=-\frac{\sqrt{1-\epsilon}\,q_g}{\omega},\qquad
U(\phi_0,q)=\frac{\epsilon q^2}{2}
 +\frac{(1-\epsilon)(q-q_g)^2}{2}.
$$

Thus the initial stress, wall jets and shared geometry do not select
omega. The later dynamics change with it. For weak amplitude zeta,
the local core approximation requires small $\zeta^2$ and

$$
h=\frac{H_\Lambda}{\omega\zeta^2},\qquad
H_\Lambda=\sqrt{\kappa\rho_v/3}.
$$

With zeta=.1 and the same parent vacuum 9/32, omega 20 gives h=1.53093,
outside the small-h approximation. The explicitly different
common-frequency member omega 2000 gives h=.0153093. It is chosen
for this hierarchy, not fitted to data. P003 retains omega 20; the
comparison does not rule out relativistic structures there.
The wall radii remain 1 and 1.9, with common middle mass .12. All
numbers use those same atlas coordinate units, with no selected SI ruler.

In local coordinates $\rho=\omega\zeta r$ and $\tau=\omega\zeta^2t$,

$$
i\partial_\tau\psi=-\frac{1}{2}\Delta\psi
 +(V-h^2\rho^2/2)\psi,\qquad \Delta V=\frac{1}{2}|\psi|^2.
$$

The h=0 nodeless core, with eigenvalue -1/2, satisfies

$$
S''+2S'/\rho+sS=0,\qquad
s''+2s'/\rho+S^2=0,\qquad s(\infty)=-1.
$$

Regularity and decay give $S(0)=1.02149303621$ and radial mass norm
$I=3.50532970334$. The 99%-mass radius is 5.67468871483 in rho.
Its virial identity is checked independently. Scaling the shape
fixes an isolated mass-radius relation,

$$
\mathfrak{M} R_f\omega^2=\frac{I\rho_f}{2},
\qquad \mathfrak{M}=GM.
$$

The core mass and frequency remain inputs; its dimensionless shape
is calculated. In the isolated problem, symmetric decreasing
rearrangement lowers the energy at fixed norm; this is the setting
of [Lieb's minimizing-solution theorem](https://doi.org/10.1002/sapm197757293).
Norm-preserving dilation gives energy $b^2T+bW$ with a finite minimum
at $b=-W/(2T)$. This explains the reference's spherical form and size
without establishing nonspherical stability in the full atlas.
Gaussian and super-Gaussian clouds with equal initial
norm evolve to tau 600. Their late density profiles, averaged over
$500\leq\tau\leq600$ and normalized by remaining mass, differ from the
independent coherent shape by 3.77% and 4.11%, compared with initial
discrepancies near 80%. Residual oscillations remain.

![Gravitational cooling and selected core shape](../docs/figures/nsc-atlas-scalar-core.png)

*Figure 19. Left: two distinct initial cloud shapes approach the

Right: scalar norm leaves the central region during relaxation.
This is a finite local envelope calculation, not the full evolving
parent and membrane history.*

The six independent runs use six worker processes. Outgoing current
at radius 20 checks loss from the core independently of the outer
absorber. Fine-run energy-accounting errors stay below $3.4\times10^{-6}$;
moving the outer boundary changes the late inner mass by about .165%.
A free-field control recovers analytic dispersion. The
[compact record](../results/summaries/nsc-atlas-scalar-core-v1.json)
keeps numerical discrepancies distinct from the weak-field, ambient
and relativistic approximations. The original cloud and its energy
are supplied, so this does not generate a primordial seed from nothing.

### 14.2. The coherent core and its inherited environment

<!-- nsc-claim:scalar-core-atlas-admission -->

The Gaussian late inner mass selects a coherent representative with
$\zeta_{\rm core}=.0826621713254$. It is the nodeless representative
of that mass, not the full radiating snapshot. Add its displacement
f to the inherited parent field, and taper its very small tail
smoothly to zero before the material wall. Retain zero scalar
momentum and homogeneous parent slice expansion v=HR.

The gravitational constraint must include the ambient scalar.
Writing $m=M(R)+\kappa U_0R^3/6$, with $U_0=21/32$, gives

$$
\beta^2=1+H^2R^2-\frac{\kappa U_0R^2}{3}-\frac{2M(R)}{R},
$$

$$
M'=\frac{\kappa R^2}{4}\beta^2f'^2
 +\frac{\kappa R^2}{2}U'_0f
 +\frac{\kappa R^2}{4}\omega^2f^2.
$$

The first and last terms are the gradient and quadratic core
contributions. The middle term is coherent interference with the
same inherited field. It cannot be discarded as if the core were
an independently added fluid.

M(0)=0 gives a regular pole. Outside the core M becomes constant;
the unsquared Israel condition then fixes the outer-wall speed and
H. Solving that condition with the mass equation rebuilds the large
parent cap and common middle. Both wall conditions and the middle
seams agree. The positive initial lapse realizes the temporal jets.
Removing the core recovers the same-frequency reference and extrinsic curvature.

| Coherent turning-point phase | Geometric mass offset | Outer-wall areal speed change |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Positive relative displacement | .0000957140113 | .000361840526 |
| Opposite relative displacement | .0000494933577 | .000187115382 |

Both have the same isolated leading mass .0000724395411. The exact
potential difference explains the main change:

$$
U(\phi_0+f)-U(\phi_0-f)=2U'_0f.
$$

The cross contribution has magnitude .0000231104644. The gradient
contribution also changes slightly through the metric. The positive-phase
proper slice energy excess is .00241200566080; it is distinct from the
geometric mass converted to energy units. Canonical absolute energy
remains nonnegative even where an excess relative to the background
is negative.

Independent field quadrature and proper-distance integration check
the mass. Sampled Hamilton residuals stay below $2\times10^{-8}$,
with wall and middle residuals near floating precision. The
[source derivation](../docs/nsc-atlas-scalar-core.md) and
[computed admission](../results/summaries/nsc-atlas-scalar-core-v1.json)
specify the complete initial construction.

The changed wall speed compares jointly prepared initial slices;
it is not a wall response propagated by the local cooling run. The
homogeneous reference's future shielding theorem is not asserted
for this new core geometry. A single formation history must also contain the
outgoing radiation, ambient-field interaction and moving walls.
This calculation supplies a profile-selection candidate and its
sourced initial admission. It leaves cloud origin, coupling and
relative-phase selection, nonlinear global evolution and observed
abundance as further physical connections.

### 14.3. Supplying a local core from the inherited parent

<!-- nsc-claim:compensated-core-reservoir -->

The core-only admission adds geometric mass. A local redistribution instead
needs a reservoir and the outgoing scalar field in the same geometry. Keep
the previous coherent core, the original parent expansion and the same
frequency-2000 action. Surround the core with a depleted scalar annulus and
a compact outward-current pulse. Determine the depletion by requiring zero
enclosing mass offset. The pulse and reservoir profiles are specified initial
data; no formation history is silently imported from the isolated cooling run.

With future normal $n$, outward unit spatial vector $s$, define $v=n(R)$,
$\beta=s(R)$, $\Pi=n(\phi)$ and $F=d\phi/dR$. The exact spherical constraints
in the convention $K=-\mathcal L_n h/2$ reduce to

$$
K_t=-\frac{v}{R},\qquad K_r=-v_R-\frac{\kappa R}{2}\Pi F,
\qquad M_R=\frac{\kappa R^2}{2}(\rho-U_0-v\Pi F),
$$

where $\rho=(\Pi^2+\beta^2F^2)/2+U$, $U_0=21/32$ and

$$
m=\frac{R}{2}(1+v^2-\beta^2)
=M+\frac{\kappa U_0R^3}{6}.
$$

Here $m$ is geometric mass, equal to $G$ times physical mass for $c=1$.
Fix $v=H_0R$. The pulse requires the displayed radial extrinsic-curvature
correction; retaining $K_r=-H_0$ there would violate the momentum constraint.

The core ends at $R_c=.120974320414$. A C4 bump depletes the inherited
displacement $\chi_0=.000433012702$ on $[R_c,2R_c]$. A sinusoidal pulse
with peak envelope $.02\chi_0$ occupies $[3R_c,4R_c]$ and has
$\Pi=-\beta F$. Its outward current is $j=\beta^2F^2$. The potential
is retained, so this instantaneous characteristic choice is not a claim
of purely outward massive propagation at all later times.

Solving $M(4R_c)=0$ fixes the maximum displacement reduction to
18.5913687%. The mass contributions are

| Region | Geometric excess, atlas units |
| --- | ---: |
| Coherent core | .0000957140112793 |
| Ambient reservoir | -.0000964548506037 |
| Outward-current pulse | .000000740839324389 |
| Enclosing offset | zero within $2\times10^{-11}$ |

The canonical absolute energy obeys $\rho\geq\rho_v=9/32$ throughout.
Depletion concerns excitation above that floor. Both original walls retain
their initial speed, acceleration and full junction data. Local geometry
does change: the maximum compactness excess $2M/R$ is about .00687441,
and the parent's proper length increases by .000244008403. Equal enclosing
mass does not imply identical internal geometry or proper energy integrals.

### 14.4. A perturbed centre and the same evolving cosmology

<!-- nsc-claim:compensated-parent-domain -->

At $R_b=4R_c$, the perturbation and its matching derivatives vanish,
$\Pi=0$ and $M=0$. Outside this sphere the spatial metric, extrinsic
curvature and scalar data equal the original parent exactly. This makes
the causal connection stronger than the earlier core-only admission.

Use the homogeneous reference angle $\theta$, with
$a_0=6.62607707610$, $\theta_b=\arcsin(R_b/a_0)=.0730942965661$ and
$\theta_w=2.85076435977$. Its future conformal-distance bound remains
$\eta_*=.515418396612$. Local Einstein--scalar uniqueness gives the
reference evolution on $\theta_b+\eta(t)<\theta<\theta_w-\eta(t)$.
The fixed annulus

$$
.588512693178<\theta<2.335345963158
$$

therefore stays in that domain for all future reference proper time.
It occupies at least 86.6472% of the **actual initial parent volume**.
The bound concerns a smooth exterior domain; it does not assume the same
light cones inside the perturbed core or establish a complete moving-wall
continuation. Any compatible causal development agrees on this domain.

For $L_{\rm ref}=a_0$ fixed by the retained geometry, its inherited vacuum
component remains

$$
\Lambda_{\rm vac}L_{\rm ref}^2
=\kappa\rho_v a_0^2=12.3482523989285.
$$

The excited scalar still contributes to the total stress. Rearranging it
into local structure does not change the inherited vacuum floor. This
establishes coexistence of a nonlinear local source and a connected,
evolving surrounding cosmology. It does not determine the observed Lambda
or an observed dark-matter abundance.

The dynamical connection must keep flux and pressure work together.
[Hayward's spherical mass law](https://arxiv.org/html/gr-qc/9710089) gives

$$
n(m)=\frac{\kappa R^2}{2}(\Pi\beta^2F-vp_r),
\qquad p_r=\frac{\Pi^2+\beta^2F^2}{2}-U.
$$

For the supplied outward characteristic, the fixed-areal-radius derivative
$D=n-(v/\beta)s$ satisfies

$$
D(m)=-\frac{\kappa R^2}{2}\beta F^2(\beta+v)^2\leq0.
$$

Where the fixed-radius trajectory is timelike, normalize $D$ to its proper
time. At the sampled peak current its mass rate is approximately
$-3.81559\times10^{-5}$. An expanding normal sphere has a different rate
because pressure does work. The [connected derivation](../docs/nsc-atlas-core-reservoir.md)
and [checks](../results/summaries/nsc-atlas-core-reservoir-v1.json) retain
both contributions. The next task is to evolve this compensated preparation
and calculate a local signal, preserving the source, outgoing field and
same-action geometry throughout.

### 14.5. Evolving the inherited field and its geometry together

<!-- nsc-claim:coupled-core-parent-evolution -->

The compensated slice supplies a full finite spherical evolution.
The scalar, its ambient reservoir and radiation source the local metric
throughout. The earlier envelope profile is an initial control; it does
not replace the Einstein--Klein--Gordon evolution equation. The action,
frequency 2000, regional flux and inherited vacuum remain fixed.

Use areal radius and the parent expansion H(t) in the metric

$$
ds^2=-\alpha^2dt^2+
\frac{(dR-\alpha HR\,dt)^2}{B}+R^2d\Omega^2,
\qquad B=1+H^2R^2-\frac{2m}{R}.
$$

This fixes angular extrinsic curvature $K_t=-H$, while the radial
curvature, mass and lapse respond to the source. With $F=\phi_R$ and
$\Pi=n(\phi)$, the momentum and Hamilton constraints give

$$
K_r=-H-\frac{\kappa R}{2}\Pi F,\qquad
m_R=\frac{\kappa R^2}{2}(\rho-HR\Pi F).
$$

The angular Einstein equation preserves that slicing by determining

$$
B\alpha_R=\dot H R+
\alpha\left(H^2R+\frac{m}{R^2}+\frac{\kappa R}{2}p_r\right).
$$

The lapse is normalized in the untouched parent. Central proper time
is calculated from it. The scalar evolves by its unaveraged wave equation
on this metric, and the same scalar drives the reference H(t). The
[equations and implementation](../docs/nsc-atlas-core-evolution.md) retain
the shift, lapse gradient, radial momentum and regular-centre limit.
The [finite record](../results/summaries/nsc-atlas-core-evolution-v1.json)
binds the model inputs and numerical controls.

Independent of the radial constraint, the geometric mass obeys

$$
m_t=\frac{\alpha\kappa R^2}{2}
\left[(B+H^2R^2)\Pi F-HR(\Pi^2+BF^2)\right].
$$

Its time integral is compared with reconstructed mass at six fixed
radii. Both expressions subtract the evolving homogeneous density.
No absorber deletes radiation, and expansion pressure work is retained.

A smooth moving radial map keeps the core at fixed physical resolution
and carries the outer grid with the parent. The boundary's initial
angle .648103368884 lies in the permanently protected reference annulus.
It can therefore receive the exact homogeneous data without introducing
an artificial reflecting wall. This connects the local evolution to an
actual parent domain while leaving the complete material-wall future open.

The calculation reuses the
[spherical oscillaton literature](https://arxiv.org/html/0710.4131),
[regular-centre methods](https://arxiv.org/html/gr-qc/0401113) and
[Lawson's integrating-factor approach](https://doi.org/10.1137/0704033).
The latter integrates the fast scalar rotation exactly within the time
step, so numerical damping of that oscillator is not interpreted as cooling.
The new connection is the sourced inherited preparation and its observer,
rather than a new generic scalar-gravity equation.

### 14.7. Conservation selects a second local scale

The image-delay relation in Section 15.6 raises a physical question: can the inherited
field support a structure much smaller than its cosmological parent?
Changing a unit rescales both. Changing the scalar frequency changes the
physical model. The frequency family already constructed above holds
$\kappa=1$, mixing $\epsilon=1/4$, fluxes $(.5,1,1.5)$, charge $.5$ and
tension $.2$ fixed. Its common initial scalar is at the grandchild minimum.
The parent vacuum, excited density and available excitation are then

$$
\Lambda_v=\kappa U_v=\frac{9}{32},\qquad
U_0=\frac{21}{32},\qquad E_b=U_0-U_v=\frac{3}{8}.
$$

The original initial wall data and parent reference radius are independent
of frequency. The later scalar history depends on it. The following
preparations use the existing coherent core shape, a depleted ambient
annulus and zero scalar normal momentum. They contain no added outgoing
pulse and are distinct from the previously evolved diffuse lens.

Let $x=\omega\zeta R$, let $A(x)$ be the retained core shape with its cutoff
at $x=20$, and write

$$
f=\sqrt{2/\kappa}\,\zeta^2A(x),\qquad
\mathfrak{M}=\frac{\zeta}{\omega}N(x).
$$

The ambient scalar displacement is $d/\omega$, with $d=\sqrt{3}/2$.
If $C$ is the original parent spatial curvature, its exact initial mass
constraint becomes

$$
N'=-\zeta^4x(A')^2N
+\frac{x^2}{2}\left[A^2+\zeta^2(A')^2\right]
+\frac{x^2\sqrt{\kappa/2}\,dA}{\omega\zeta^2}
-\frac{Cx^4(A')^2}{2\omega^2}.
$$

The coefficient multiplying $N$ does not depend on frequency. With a
regular centre this proves, for the supplied shape,

$$
\mathfrak{M}_c=\frac{\zeta N_0(20)}{\omega}
+\frac{\zeta N_1(20)}{\omega^2}
+\frac{\zeta N_2(20)}{\omega^3}.
$$

This exact decomposition is not a fitted scaling curve. Its coefficients
retain self-gravity, interference with the inherited ambient field, and
parent curvature. Geometric excess mass is measured above the excited
homogeneous parent; in physical units it is $GM_c/c^2$.

A reservoir cannot supply arbitrary positive core mass. For zero initial
scalar momentum, the positive radial chart gives

$$
\mathfrak{M}_R=\frac{\kappa R^2}{2}
\left(\frac{BF^2}{2}+U-U_0\right)
\geq-\frac{\kappa E_bR^2}{2}.
$$

Reducing the core mass to zero at the reservoir's outer radius therefore
requires

$$
R_d^3-R_c^3\geq\frac{6\mathfrak{M}_c}{\kappa E_b},\qquad
R_c=\frac{20}{\omega\zeta}.
$$

Since $\kappa E_b=4\Lambda_v/3$ for this inherited state, every smooth
finite-energy match in the stated domain satisfies

$$
\frac{\Lambda_v(R_d^3-R_c^3)}{\mathfrak{M}_c}>\frac{9}{2}.
$$

Equality would require the scalar to remain at its parent minimum almost
everywhere through the annulus, contradicting continuous matching to the
excited ambient field at its ends. This lower bound does not depend on a
chosen depletion profile. Its coefficient belongs to the declared inherited
excitation/vacuum ratio, rather than to every possible scalar state.

The core mass falls as $\omega^{-1}$, whereas a reservoir with a fixed
ratio $R_d/R_c$ has available excitation falling as $\omega^{-3}$.
For $R_d=2R_c$ and the retained $\zeta=.0826621713$, the resulting
necessary frequency ceiling is $6221.94$. Above it, even full removal
of the excitation from that annulus cannot supply the core. Gradient
energy strengthens the restriction. The [full derivation](../docs/nsc-atlas-scale-hierarchy.md)
also extends the inequality to arbitrary scalar momentum where $B\geq v^2$.

Allowing the reservoir radius to respond resolves the obstruction. Choose

$$
\chi(R)=\frac{d}{\omega}[1-a b(x)],\qquad
x=\frac{R-R_c}{R_d-R_c},\qquad b(x)=1024x^5(1-x)^5,
$$

with supplied depletion $0<a\leq1$. The scalar returns smoothly to its
original parent value at both joins. Solve the exact constraint for $R_d$
so that the exterior geometric mass offset vanishes. Outside this radius the original initial
parent geometry and material-wall matching data are recovered exactly.
The independent proper-distance Hamilton equation checks the matching.

For fixed core amplitude and depletion, put $R_d=z\omega^{-1/3}$ and
use the positive leading mass coefficient $C_m=\zeta N_0(20)$. The scaled endpoint constraint
tends to

$$
0=1-\frac{\kappa d^2z^3}{4C_m}J(a),\qquad
J(a)=\int_0^1 x^2[2ab(x)-a^2b(x)^2]dx>0.
$$

Its positive root has nonzero derivative. The implicit-function theorem
continues it to sufficiently large finite frequency. Provided the limiting
core retains a positive radial-chart margin, the reservoir also remains
regular and lies inside the parent. The finite examples check these
conditions. This yields the hierarchy

$$
R_c\sim\omega^{-1},\qquad R_d\sim\omega^{-1/3},\qquad
R_d/R_c\sim\omega^{2/3},\qquad R_d\ll L_{\rm ref}.
$$

The parent vacuum and original initial wall geometry remain fixed. Each
member retains a causally protected parent annulus under the previously
derived energy-floor bound. These are initial-constraint and causal-domain
results; formation, persistence and the complete coupled wall future are
separate calculations.

For the declared half-depletion profile,

The following section evolves this two-scale preparation in a leading
